#12022 1 2006-10-31T11:43:33Z Red Reamer
The signature makers have banded together, in one thread, and now offer sigs to those who want them. If anyone wants a sig, ask, using the format below. Then, one of the team will make it for you. Hell, you might even get more than one.

The format:
Quote: Sig Request
Primary colours:
Theme:
Renders/images:
Text:
Complexity:
Matching avatar (yes or no):
Dimensions (size):
Preferred artist (optional):
(Just a note, on the preferred artist part; I'd really hope that it is a rarely used option. In fact, I wasn't going to include it, but hopefully the (optional) qualifier dissuades people from constantly opting for a particular artist. Unless you have a really good reason, please don't ask.)

Important! To prevent favouritism or any other biases, this thread works on a first come first served basis. This post will be edited and updated to include the requests that have and haven't been made. So it doesn't matter if more than one sig is made for one person, as long as at least one is made, before moving on to the next request.

Happy sigging,
Kash.

-IMPORTANT- Update: We're pretty much doing it anyway, but I thought I'd make it official. Anything you want made; request it. We'll do our best.

#256567 93 2006-11-18T18:37:53Z Bigblargh
Now presenting: The Sig Request From Hell!

I'm not sure if I'm allowed to do this one, because it specifies things beyond the criteria you
normally fill out. Ah well. It's not as scary as it looks.

Slaps and I are halves, and therefore, we must have matching sigs. They are very similar, so you can skim the second description, but important differences are bolded.

BIGBLARGH'S SIG

There is a room. You can see the top right corner of it, where the brick walls meet the cement ceiling. There is an lightbulb that is flickering (animated) with a red glow, illuminating the room and a piece of graffiti in the middle of the wall that proclaims "BIGBLARGH" in red rounded bubble letters (meaning curvy letters with white outside, kinda like eggs). The wall itself should have cracks, missing bricks, and maybe a cobweb. Make sure the cobweb casts a shadow if it's in the right place. Try to animate the sig with the light more often off than on.

SLAPPYMCGEE's SIG

There is a room. You can see the top left corner of it, where the brick walls meet the cement ceiling. There is an lightbulb that is flickering (animated) with a blue glow, illuminating the room and a piece of graffiti in the middle of the wall that proclaims "SLAPPYMCGEE" in blue pointed lightning-like bubble letters (meaning curvy letters with white outside, kinda like eggs). The wall itself should have cracks, missing bricks, and maybe a cobweb. Make sure the cobweb casts a shadow if it's in the right place. Try to animate the sig with the light more often off than on.

Primary colours: A dark brick color, as the room is made of bricks.
Theme: Hmm... grunge, I suppose.
Renders/images: A lightbulb, graffiti, maybe a cobweb.
Complexity: Perhaps not complex, but very specific.
Matching avatar (yes or no): No thanks.
Dimensions (size): Anything sane.
Preferred artist (optional): The artist doesn't matter, but I wrote this with Red Reamer in mind because I would love to see this with his style.

Take as long as you want.

#274791 225 2006-12-22T03:26:58Z Navick
Quote: numa_ninja, December 21, 2006 06:15 pm
Thanks CTB . What I can't figure out is how to put my sig onto my signature thing?

If you use Internet Explorer:

1. Right-click on the image.
2. Click on Properties.
3. Copy the image location (which, in this case, would be http://img50.imageshack.us/img50/6899/numasigcopyvj8.png.)
4. Open up My Controls, and go into Edit Signature.
5. Click the IMG button (found here).
6. Paste the image link into the box, and click Ok.
7. Click Update My Signature.

If you use Firefox:

1. Right-click on the image.
2. Click on Copy Image Location.
3. Open up My Controls, and go into Edit Signature.
5. Click the IMG button (found here).
6. Paste the image link into the box, and click Ok.
7. Click Update My Signature.

Those are the only internet browsers that I know how to navigate. If you use something else, I can't really help you there.

#310374 560 2007-04-06T12:45:23Z catspride

#310393 561 2007-04-06T14:48:53Z Navick
Frets, here's a guide.

Jakel's 'How to put images in your signature' (By Jakel)

I have this tutorial for two different web browsers: Firefox and Internet Explorer.

If you use Firefox:[/b]
1. Host an image at Photobucket or Imageshack.
2. Right-click on the uploaded image, and click on "Copy Image Location". This will now send the image link to your clipboard.
3. Go to the "My Controls" link at the top of the page, and click "Edit my Signature".
4. Press the "[IMG]"[/img] button at the top of the text box. After that, a pop-up should, well, pop-up. Press "Ctrl+V" (For Windows), or right-click and click on "Paste" in the text line, and press "OK". If you look back into the signature text box, it should say something like: 
[IMG]http://someimageuploadingsite.com/somenumberandlettercombination/thenameofthefilethatyouuploaded.somefilettype
5. Press "Update My Signature". It should now show an image above the signature text box.

You have now just put an image into your signature! Congratulations!

If you use Internet Explorer:[/b]
1. Host an image at Photobucket or Imageshack.
2. Right-click on the uploaded image, and click on "Properties", and a box should pop up.
3. Highlight the long image URL, and copy it (Ctrl+C or right click and press "Copy"). This will now send the image link to your clipboard.
4. Go to the "My Controls" link at the top of the page, and click "Edit my Signature".
5. Press the "[IMG]"[/img] button at the top of the text box. After that, a pop-up should, well, pop-up. Press "Ctrl+V" (For Windows), or right-click and click on "Paste" in the text line, and press "OK". If you look back into the signature text box, it should say something like: 
[IMG]http://someimageuploadingsite.com/somenumberandlettercombination/thenameofthefilethatyouuploaded.somefilettype
6. Press "Update My Signature". It should now show an image above the signature text box.

You have now just put an image into your signature! Congratulations!

If you have a problem while doing this, please personal message me.

#315074 610 2007-04-22T19:43:13Z Navick
brocerius, here's a guide.

Jakel's 'How to put images in your signature' (By Jakel)

I have this tutorial for two different web browsers: Firefox and Internet Explorer.

If you use Firefox:[/b]
1. Host an image at Photobucket or Imageshack.
2. Right-click on the uploaded image, and click on "Copy Image Location". This will now send the image link to your clipboard.
3. Go to the "My Controls" link at the top of the page, and click "Edit my Signature".
4. Press the "[IMG]"[/img] button at the top of the text box. After that, a pop-up should, well, pop-up. Press "Ctrl+V" (For Windows), or right-click and click on "Paste" in the text line, and press "OK". If you look back into the signature text box, it should say something like: 
[IMG]http://someimageuploadingsite.com/somenumberandlettercombination/thenameofthefilethatyouuploaded.somefilettype
5. Press "Update My Signature". It should now show an image above the signature text box.

You have now just put an image into your signature! Congratulations!

If you use Internet Explorer:[/b]
1. Host an image at Photobucket or Imageshack.
2. Right-click on the uploaded image, and click on "Properties", and a box should pop up.
3. Highlight the long image URL, and copy it (Ctrl+C or right click and press "Copy"). This will now send the image link to your clipboard.
4. Go to the "My Controls" link at the top of the page, and click "Edit my Signature".
5. Press the "[IMG]"[/img] button at the top of the text box. After that, a pop-up should, well, pop-up. Press "Ctrl+V" (For Windows), or right-click and click on "Paste" in the text line, and press "OK". If you look back into the signature text box, it should say something like: 
[IMG]http://someimageuploadingsite.com/somenumberandlettercombination/thenameofthefilethatyouuploaded.somefilettype
6. Press "Update My Signature". It should now show an image above the signature text box.

You have now just put an image into your signature! Congratulations!

If you have a problem while doing this, please personal message me.

Umm, I'd like to make a new request.

This time, the (not sig, banner for my forum, has to be 63 pixels high) image will have a flame design like the first "Dusty" sig, in blue like the one at the top of the Fast Reply bar, and a picture of the ninja in the middle of the victory pose where he kicks out and raises his arm. Text should say "Dusty."
That pose is kinda hard to get a screenshot of, but I figured out how. Start out at the menu of N, with the demo playing. Click the \ key at the beginning of the level, turn capslock on, play the level till you either beat it or die. Repeat several times. Then, when the game is sufficiently lagging, win a level and turn capslock off. Click to cycle through the victory poses till you find the one I'm looking for. It will be slowed down enough that you can take a screenshot of the middle of the pose.
Hope that helps

Thanks!

By dimensions they mean the size of the image. The length and width.
For instance, 100x200 is a tiny rectangular image.
If you have MS Paint on your computer, you can open it up, make the "White Box" the same size as you'd like your sig to be and then hit Ctrl+E to open Attributes.

You should then see something similiar to this=

==Implying rules as of now, important read==


Ok, this is necessary, due to the amount of spam we've recently got in this thread, I'm taking action.
  • If a request isn't completed within a week, someone.
  • If that someone doesn't reply, someone else.
  • After that, be patient, wait a while (Approx another 3 days), then PM the first member again.
  • After this, leave it, your request probably won't be answered, this may be to a few reasons, insults etc. the member might be away, so as a notice to artists, post your absence so this stage isn't reached.
Thank you for your time.

~Chase

#352922 958 2007-08-09T04:26:40Z southpaw
Quote: incluye, August 09, 2007 01:59 am
okay, this is driving me nuts. How do you guys make those backgrounds? I got the pictures of Sora just fine. But I can't make the backgrounds.
Just PM me with some help. Or tell me how. Better yet, give me a step-by-step guide.

Step 1: Go to deviantArt.com

Step 2: Find a brush set that says "Abstract" somewhere within the title

Step 3: Download and add to your brushes folder

Step 4: Experiment

Step 5: Practice patience. You might get a good background on your first try, but the next ten will be more difficult, and then they will get easier (I approximated the numbers, but you get my gist)

That is really all there is to it. Imaging is all about practice, patience, and persistence.

Then invert the whole thing, and apply the same brushes. They will look different. Mess around with the opacity of brushes as well, as some brushes do come in a very dark color.

#353110 963 2007-08-09T17:21:15Z southpaw
@Incluye:

Go to the folder you saved the original GIMP files in. There might be a folder that says Presets in the GIMP folder. Click that and there might be a folder that says Brushes. Click that. Put your downloaded stuff there. At least that is how it is for Photoshop, but GIMP might be different, although I would imagine their file setup is quite similar.

Okay, figured it out. How do I use new brushes with GIMP?

For me:

C: Drive (or whatever your hard drive is called) -> Program Files -> GIMP 2.0 or something -> share -> gimp -> 2.0 -> brushes

When you open the brushes folder, all of your brushes will be there. Drag and drop your brushes into that folder. Restart GIMP and the brushes will be there.
